# ATT&CK evidence coverage and correlation candidates

Generated from raw dataset bytes by `python -m src.preprocessing.evidence_inventory`. The input and output SHA-256 values are retained in `inventory-manifest.json`. This is Phase 2 inspection; no normalization, ingestion, final technique selection or detection rule is implemented.

## Counting scope

The nonduplicated event corpus carries **24 parent-technique** and **21 sub-technique** Wazuh labels. Across all structured values, documentation and publisher code, there are **112 parent-shaped** and **172 sub-technique-shaped** identifiers. The latter includes metadata, assessment references and text examples, not confirmed occurrences.

- Event counts use one nonduplicated corpus; label sets can overlap across techniques.
- A Wazuh label is an annotation, not independent evidence of successful malicious execution.
- Manifest expectations, SCA references, code and documentation are not counted as event detections.
- Pairs involve distinct documents and known UTC Windows event times; no detection rule is created.
- Scenario fields are derived labels, not independent event-level benign/malicious ground truth.

Names below come only from unambiguous one-ID/one-name source rows. Unresolved names are left blank; co-labelled source names are retained separately in the machine-readable evidence. Tactic lists describe co-labels, not inferred assignments.

Event-level labels establish what the dataset annotates. They do not by themselves establish successful malicious execution. The evidence examples distinguish actual observed processes, file changes, module loads and network activity. For example, a task scheduler DLL load is weaker evidence than an observed task registration; a file-deletion command line is evidence of an attempted action rather than proof that the file was deleted. No final three techniques are chosen.

## Candidate suitability

These simple ratings measure analytical usability, not accuracy. Volume: Strong >=1,000, Moderate >=100; field coverage: Strong >=80%, Moderate >=25% have command/file/object/module/network detail. Evidence quality is at most Moderate because original rule labels remain unvalidated. Ground truth and benign comparison are Weak without independent labels. SIEM usability is Strong at >=100 rows and >=80% detail. Correlation is Strong with >=10 exclusive-label structural pair observations within 300 seconds, Moderate with any candidate relation. Dashboard usefulness is Strong at >=100 rows with detail. Each reason gives the underlying counts.

## Correlation shortlist

Candidates are sorted by exclusive-label process-GUID/parent-child pair observations within 300 seconds; this is a structural shortlist, not an automatic technique selection. Only distinct source documents with an explicit Windows UTC timestamp and computer are eligible. For non-parent relationships the algorithm chooses the nearest preceding event per other label in each context. It tests 60/300/900-second windows. Pair counts are neither all possible joins nor detection alerts; relation categories overlap. Zero time differences are ties, not proven ordering. Host/user/time or scenario agreement alone does not imply causality. The exclusive-label column requires A without B on one document and B without A on the other. It helps expose repeated co-labelling; it does not independently validate either behaviour.

### Repeated co-labels requiring caution

| Labels | Structural observations &lt;=300s | Exclusive-label observations &lt;=300s |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ["T1059.003", "T1574.001"] | 1106 | 0 |
| ["T1574.001", "T1574.002"] | 281 | 0 |

Even distinct documents can repeat the same pair of labels. These combinations do not establish two separately observed behaviours.

Shared derived scenarios: S07: 3951; S16: 58; S28: 118; S33: 38; S34: 4358; S36: 508. Computers: desktop-ds4fbf4: 228; desktop-fmi6mml: 2721; desktop-qgb4eqh: 12415.

Eligible individual documents carrying both labels: 0. These single documents are never counted as a two-event correlation.

Potential: an exact actor GUID or parent/child GUID ties separate observations to process context; the later phase can test whether the supporting event improves a baseline. DLL-load and routine administrative contexts may still be benign. The scenario assignments are derived from overlapping technique sets.

Interpretation of the displayed source examples: Shell ancestry is observable, but the displayed child updater.exe -Command exit does not itself show deletion despite its source label. Review full commands and independent file events before treating this pair as deletion evidence.
